## Changes 2026-11-11: Retro pixel and Office people as Characters choices in every world (T225, owner decision; FR-320..FR-326, T290..T296; issue #1399)

**Status: spec only.** Nothing here is built; no `src/` change. Six questions (C2..C7) are owner answers of 2026-11-11 (below, and as comments on PR #1528). **C1 and C8 are proposals**, not owner decisions (review of #1528, 2026-11-14): no owner words for them were found in a trusted source, so each is marked *proposal, pending owner confirmation*. What stays open: the owner's confirmation of C1 and C8 and his approval of each new picture (FR-326, T296; the pictures were approved on 2026-11-13, below the PR comment of that date, and are still not committed).

**Decided** (owner, Vikram, Telegram 2026-11-11 09:27 UTC, option 2 of the question in the T261 delta, PR #1512): T225 is built as app code, not as a driver over what exists. (a) **Retro pixel** becomes a Characters choice (Settings > Characters), behind the `retro` flag, so Retro pixel people can be shown in the Photo office; (b) **Office people** can be picked inside the arcade, by FR-200's "any set works in any world", instead of only through the closed-flag fallback that draws the Photo office.

**What exists (FR-031 check, read on `main` at `80c825633`).**

- `crew.js` already has the row: `REAL_SETS` lists `{ id: 'retro', name: 'Retro pixel', choice: 'retro', flag: 'retro' }`, and `peopleFor` resolves the choice `retro` to the Retro pixel set when the flag is on; `levelFor('retro')` is `full` or `closed`. The seam needs no new branch for (a).
- What drops it: `CHARACTER_CHOICES` (`src/shared/characters.ts`) and `CHARACTERS` (`src/renderer/theme.js`) do not list `retro`, so `parseCharacters` drops it in main (`settings:setCharacters`, `src/main/settings.ts`) and in the renderer; the Characters list in `screens.js` and `CHARACTER_NAMES` in `app.js` do not show it.
- Office people have no Characters value: their `REAL_SETS` row has the empty choice, so in the arcade the default (`theme`, "World's default") draws Retro pixel people and nothing else picks Office people. (b) needs a new value.
- The spec named the Flat value `pixel` (FR-214, FR-266, Q10, T122, T227); `crew.js` calls the set `retro`. **Resolved on #1676's review (2026-11-14): the stored Characters value is `pixel` (imported as `PIXEL_CHARACTERS` from `src/shared/characters.ts`), the set stays `retro`, and `peopleFor` maps the one to the other in one place (the `REAL_SETS` row's `choice`); T344 stays a future rename.** First found at build: **Main has since built T227 with `pixel`** (#1696, 2026-11-13: `PIXEL_CHARACTERS = 'pixel'`, `migrateRetroTheme` writes it with the `retroMovePending` marker; `pixel` is read back only once T122 lists it), behind the closed flag, so files migrated with the flag open (none ship yet, the flag is closed) could hold `pixel`. C1 *proposes* `retro`; T227's done text stays as history and the new task T344 changes the code, tests and mutants (FR-266 and the others keep `pixel` as written until the owner confirms).
- The Retro pixel set (`sets/retro/`, 1.2 MB, 15 people, 18 frames each, `pixelArt: true`, lossless WebP, no half-size pictures) has the six seated frames, `type`, `book`, `knock`, `letter`, `counter` (from behind, facing the fixture, as Office people's standing frames are) and the `face-*` and `sit` frames. It has **no `runA` and `runB`**: the Photo office's Terminal visitor falls to `letter` (`POSE_FALLBACK`). Office people's `runA`/`runB` are seen from behind.
- A trap for the build: `realoffice.js`'s `render` merges the world and the set (`Object.assign({}, world, set)`) and reads `meta.pixelArt` for the world's pixel mode, so the Retro pixel set (`pixelArt: true`) in the Photo office would switch the Photo office's world-level pixel rules on (no grid snap of the room, `image-rendering`, the arcade's screen box). The world's pixel mode must come from the world, the people's from the set (`meta.pixelPeople`, already there).

**Requirements.**

- **FR-320** **Retro pixel is a Characters value, `pixel`** (the stored value is `pixel`, as FR-266 and main's T227 write it; the SET it draws is `retro`, `sets/retro`, mapped in one place, `REAL_SETS`' `choice`. C1 proposes renaming the value `retro`: T344, not decided, not built). In `CHARACTER_CHOICES`, `theme.js`'s list, `CHARACTER_NAMES` and the Characters choices (cards, FR-303), kept equal by `test:characters`. **Always shown** (C2): while the world flag `WORLD_FLAGS.retro` is closed its card is shown marked "Not open yet" and cannot be picked (disabled, and its name says so to a screen reader); while open it is a normal pick. **One flag:** "closed" for this choice reads the same `WORLD_FLAGS.retro` as FR-301's world card (`levelFor('pixel')`), never its own copy, so the world card and this card cannot disagree; T230 removes the flag and with it the "Not open yet" state. In every Real office world it draws the Retro pixel set through `peopleFor` (FR-202), the one seam; no other set's pack is requested (FR-205, SC-018). In Flat it draws Retro pixel heads (FR-214, heads by FR-203), built in the same work behind the same flag (C4, T122).
- **FR-321** **Office people is a Characters value.** A new explicit value, shown as **Office people**, a card right after "World's default" (C5; FR-303's order: the default first, then the sets that can draw in the world), whose `REAL_SETS` row is Office people, so the arcade, the cave and every later world can draw Office people by choice. In Flat it shows Office people's heads cropped from their pictures by `faceBox` (FR-203). `World's default` stays the first entry and keeps following the world. It changes no stored setting: a person who never picks it sees what they see today (SC-017). Its stored id is new in `CHARACTER_CHOICES` (not the empty choice that means "not picked"), named at build in spec 077's delta. **Cards, not a list:** FR-303 (merged) makes Characters picture cards in one radio group under the chosen world; FR-320..FR-322 are card behaviour (a disabled "Not open yet" card for Retro pixel, one card per set with FR-303's group picture and accessible name). **Pictures:** T265b (built as T343, PR #1705, open at this writing) makes a group picture for every set under `sets/`, so the `retro` set (value `pixel`) and Office people have one the day it lands and nothing new is drawn for them; what these two need is that FR-303's "sets that can draw in that world" (FR-202) lists them, which T291 and T292 do, and a `test:chargroups` case naming both. **Order:** T265b first (its pictures), then T266 (the cards); T291 and T292 add the two cards to whichever of T266 or the old Settings list is live when they land, so they need no ordering against T266 beyond "after T265b" and are built on the cards if T266 has landed (T266 is "with or after T230", so it may land later: then they are added to the old list in 077's pattern and T266 carries them over).
- **FR-322** **A saved Retro pixel choice whose flag is closed** (C3). The choice is kept (main, which knows no flags, keeps storing it); the world's default people are drawn meanwhile, and a line under the Characters list says so, in 097 FR-336's style: "Real office shows <set> until Retro pixel is open in Real office." (the `closed` level of `levelFor`). Flat draws the theme's own figures meanwhile. **Test (FR-035-style, named here so it is built first):** `check-characters` in Flat with a saved `pixel` and `WORLD_FLAGS.retro` closed draws the theme's own figures, keeps the stored value and shows the line; a mutant that draws the Retro pixel heads while closed fails.
- **FR-323** **The world's pixel mode is the world's.** Drawing a pixel-art set in a world without `pixelArt` changes none of that world's rules (grid snap, picture smoothing of the room, screen box); only the people are drawn with the nearest pixel. Drawing a photo set in the arcade keeps 097 T247's rule (scaled to the arcade's people, whole beside the cabinet).
- **FR-324** **Pixel size in the Photo office** (C7). Retro pixel people are scaled to the Photo office's people size with nearest-pixel scaling; slightly uneven pixels are accepted, FR-255's recorded `[KNOWN GAP]` ("drawn at that world's size with the nearest pixel, which may be uneven"), unchanged.
- **FR-325** **One choice for all worlds** (C8). FR-201 holds: an explicit pick sticks across worlds, one `characters` value in `durbar-settings.json`, through main; "World's default" is how people follow each world.
- **FR-326** **New running frames for Retro pixel people** (C6). `runA` and `runB` for each of the 15 people (30 pictures), so the Photo office's Terminal visitor runs in the same set. Art, not code (097 FR-337): no frame is faked by mirroring, tinting or reusing another frame, **and a test says so**: `test:realoffice-assets` fails when two frames of a person (or of the set) have the same pixel hash, or a `runB` is the horizontal mirror of its `runA` (mutant: a `runB` written as the mirror of `runA` fails). Spec for the pictures:
  - **Pose list:** `runA`, `runB`, a two-frame jog in place, seen from behind facing the fixture like the set's `type`, `book`, `knock`, `letter`, `counter` and like Office people's `runA`/`runB`; named `pNN-runA.webp`, `pNN-runB.webp`.
  - **Size:** the set's standing grid: the person's standing figure height (72 grid px in `set.json`'s `stand`) with feet on the same foot line, one file pixel per grid pixel, as every frame of the set; `set.json`'s `stand` poses gain both.
  - **Palette:** each person's own colours, snapped to the colours of their existing frames (no new colour), binary alpha, as `scripts/build-arcade-pack.py` and `scripts/build-arcade-sleep.py` do; pixel style kept (`docs/world-design.md`: a set keeps its own art style).
  - **Budget and flow:** at most 300 KB a picture (owner), saved as lossless WebP (`VP8L`) by the repo's pixel-pack flow, then checked by `test:realoffice-assets` (pixel-art rules and the set's caps); the set's size is measured and written in `ASSETS.md`.
  - **How:** Codex image generation on the owner's plan, under his standing approval of 2026-10-31 for office and character art, one run per person with the person's own frame as reference, as the `sit` frames were made; provenance per picture in `sets/retro/ASSETS.md`, fictional people, likeness check (097 FR-067).
  - **Gates:** nothing is generated before this spec PR is merged; **the owner approves each picture before it is committed**. Until the art lands, the `letter` fallback draws for that pose and the report names it.

**Questions (C2..C7 answered by the owner 2026-11-11, recorded on PR #1528; C1 and C8 are proposals).**

- **C1 The value's name. PROPOSAL (the coordinator, 2026-11-11), pending owner confirmation, not an owner decision:** `retro`, as `crew.js` has it. It touches FR-214, FR-266, Q10, the Q8 row, T122, T230 and main's done T227, so those keep `pixel` as written (T227 as history) until the owner confirms; T344 then changes the code. Source searched 2026-11-14: the PR comment of 2026-11-11 09:35 UTC says "decided by the coordinator"; no owner words found in #1400, #1512 or the PR.
- **C2 Where Retro pixel sits while the flag is closed. ANSWERED (Vikram, 2026-11-11 09:34 UTC, option 3):** always shown (his word was "listed", read as a card since FR-303), marked "Not open yet" and not selectable while the `retro` flag is closed (FR-320).
- **C3 A saved Retro pixel when the flag is off. ANSWERED (Vikram, 2026-11-11 09:35 UTC, option 1):** kept; the world's default people drawn with the FR-336-style line (FR-322).
- **C4 Flat and T122. ANSWERED (Vikram, 2026-11-11 09:35 UTC, option 1):** T122 is built in the same work behind the same flag, so Retro pixel means the same in every world (FR-320, T294).
- **C5 Office people as a value. ANSWERED (Vikram, 2026-11-11 09:36 UTC, option 1):** "Office people", right after "World's default"; Flat shows Office people's heads cropped from their pictures (FR-321).
- **C6 Frames Retro pixel lacks. ANSWERED (Vikram, 2026-11-11 09:37 UTC, option 3):** new `runA`/`runB` frames, 30 pictures, pixel style, an art task with its own number (FR-326, T296).

Only the chosen set loads (FR-205). Reduced motion, Motion off and forced colours as 097. New art: 30 small pixel pictures (FR-326).

## Changes 2026-11-11: the painted-pixel check and a person whose box is 0 px (T304, issue #1517, no new FR)

Linux UI shard 3/4 went red once (run 42966366604, job 130345255741, on #1510): `shots-102-arcade` failed at 15 agents, 1280x800, in FR-255's "every person whole beside the cabinet" check with `IndexSizeError: Failed to execute 'getImageData' ... The source width is 0`. The width is the canvas the check builds from the person's box (`Math.round(b.width)`), not the picture: the check already skipped a picture that was not loaded. **Cause: not established** (the person is not named in the log; declared by the author, `read:` not reproduced by the reviewer: 5 of 5 runs alone and 5 of 5 with six busy loops passed on the author's Mac). **Change (the driver only):** the painted-pixel check also treats a person whose box rounds to 0 px (width or height) as BUSY and reads again for the same bounded 5 s an unloaded picture gets; if it stays so, it fails by name (`data-for`, size, inline style). No retry beyond that; nothing weakened. `sim:` `shots-102-arcade` (a test-only `ARCADE_MUTANT=zero` makes every person 0 px wide for 1.5 s and must pass; `ARCADE_MUTANT=zero-stuck` never lets go and must fail with the named person; the old driver code plus the same injected style died with `IndexSizeError` on the author's Mac, declared by the author, not reproduced by the reviewer because the old code has no mutant). `[KNOWN GAP]` the cause of the one red is not known, and the fix is not yet seen on Linux CI.

## Changes 2026-11-11: the arcade's status-filter notice reads (T306, issue #1559; no new FR)

In the arcade the line "Showing only idle · N hidden · Show all" (spec 001 FR-180) was painted `rgb(23,20,43)` on the page `rgb(11,10,26)`, 1.09:1 at 1440x900 and 1280x800 (Clean Office 6.56:1): `html[data-ui="retro"] .filter-note{color:var(--surface)}` in `themes.css` was written for the Retro Pixel theme (cream `--surface` on a navy page) and also matched the arcade, whose `--surface` is as dark as its page. Fix: `html[data-world="retro"][data-ui="retro"]` sets the notice to `--ink-2`, its bold part and the Show all button to `--ink` (office copy 13.2:1 and 17.23:1 on the page; the List's copy, a second element on `--surface`, 12.08:1 and 15.78:1; Show all keeps its underline from `styles.css`, so it still reads as a control). The Retro Pixel theme, Clean Office, the dark fixture theme and the other worlds are unchanged (Clean Office 6.56:1 and the dark fixture 6.8:1 asserted by the driver; Retro Pixel 12.48:1 measured once by hand, no script may name that theme: `test:userthemes`). The same cause made the arcade's `.empty` lines `--surface` on `--surface` (the List's "No agents match these filters.", 1.00:1) or on `--bg` ("Opening the Real office…", 1.09:1); `html[data-world="retro"][data-ui="retro"] .empty` now takes `--muted` (List line measured 9.17:1; the loading line is 10.02:1 by token, see the gap below). The suite entry `check-status-filter retro` (`DRIVER_WORLD=retro`) measures the notice text, its bold part and Show all in the arcade at both sizes and checks Show all is underlined; its crop is saved (and was read). `FILTER_MUTANT=old` (puts the old colour back and fails with `arcade 1440x900: #filterNote contrast 1.09:1 is below 4.5:1`).

- **Driver.** `check-status-filter retro` (arcade, both sizes) measures the office notice, the List notice and the List empty line (reached by a status plus a folder with no agent of it), asserting 4.5:1, an opaque colour (alpha 1, ancestor opacity product 1) and a drawn underline; the flat behaviour loop does not run in it. Mutants, each killed by its own label: `FILTER_MUTANT=old` (`#filterNote contrast 1.09:1`), `list` (`#list .filter-note contrast 1:1`), `empty` (`#list .list-wrap>.empty contrast 1:1`).
- **[KNOWN GAP]** The contrast is the resolved colour against the first opaque background behind it (the driver's method), not painted pixels; translucency is asserted away rather than blended. "Opening the Real office…" (`.empty` while the office is built) is not reached by the driver: its 10.02:1 on `--bg` is from the tokens. The arcade's other `.empty` variants (floor empty, no agents yet) take the same colour but are not measured. The Open-office flat behaviour ("covers nothing", "above the first room") is not run in the arcade. The keyboard focus ring of "Show all" (`.fn-all`, the global `:focus-visible`) on the dark arcade floor is not measured (issue #1646; the top bar's ring is #1616). `.empty` is `--muted` in the whole arcade but only the List variant is measured.

### 2026-11-11: the arcade plate's status word and helper dots (issue #1558; changes 097 FR-016 and 097 FR-308 in the arcade; round 2 same day)

In the arcade at 1280x800 (no helper strip fits, 097 FR-308's last step) a plate with 3 helpers showed "+3 dots Compacti...": the plate's one line is `+N and dots`, then the status, and the dots took a fixed width, so the 146 px plate left 70 px for the dot and the status; "Compacting" ended 3 px past its box and the ellipsis cut the word itself (Retrying, Gave up and 1440x900, with a strip, were whole).

- **FR-311** (102's own number: spec 097 has a different FR-311, *Checks*, in the same helper block; always read it as "102 FR-311") **Priority on the arcade plate with helpers and no strip: the +N, then the status word, then the dots.** (1) The `+N` is never cut. (2) The status word (with its dot) stays whole; the task after it has no width of its own and takes what is left, with its ellipsis. (3) The helpers' dots give way: they drop out *whole*, the last first (never a sliver of a dot). (4) Only when the `+N`, one dot and the word do not fit is the word ellipsised (a KNOWN GAP, below). The word and the task are two runs of the line (`.ro-wd`, `.ro-tk`; read as text the line is as before) and the `+N` and each dot are items of a one-line wrapping row (`.ro-hc`, `.dot` in `.ro-help`, the extra items wrap out of sight). Other worlds, the Photo office's plates and the lounge seat's plate are unchanged.
- **The dots are in the heads' order.** The plate's dots now come from `orderedHelpers` (waiting and error first, as the flat office and 097 FR-306's heads), not from creation order: a waiting or error helper's mark is never clipped while an OK, working or done helper's mark shows; `+N` counts all of them. Before this the dot that went was arbitrary (the third helper, failed, was the one lost at 1280x800). The plate's `aria-label` and `title` still carry only the count (097 FR-308), so on this plate the dots are the only mark of a failed helper.
- `test:` `shots-102-arcade` (*issue 1558*), at 15 agents with 3 helpers each (the third failed) at 1440x900 and 1280x800, one load with an agent compacting, one with an agent retrying and one that gave up, and, at 1280x800, 24 agents all at their limit (the 122 px narrow cabinet's plate with a rename pencil): on every plate with helpers the Range of the `+N` lies inside the line's box, the word's Range lies inside the *plate's content box* (not under the pencil) and, outside the narrow case, inside its line, no dot is cut in part, and a waiting or error dot is never gone while another shows; at 1280x800 a plate with "Compacting" and one with "Retrying" and helpers must be drawn (not a vacuous pass), each keeps at least one dot, and the compacting plate's failed helper's red dot is shown. The 1440x900 loads draw a strip, so they have no plate with helpers (they pass trivially and say so in their log). Mutants, each killed at its own label (`ARCADE_MUTANT`): `cutword` (the old layout), `cutplate` (a plate narrower than its line), `nodots` (the mark's line gets only its `+N`), `sliver` (the dots do not wrap), `clipstate` (the dots in creation order, the error dot last).
- ~~KNOWN GAP: on the 122 px narrow cabinet's plate ... "Limit rea..."~~ closed by FR-312 below (issue #1574): the word is whole there and the failed helper is marked by the count.

### 2026-11-12: the narrow cabinet's plate: word, +N and a mark for a failed helper (issue #1574; closes the KNOWN GAP of FR-311; stacked on #1564)

The narrow cabinet's plate (the fallback plan at g = 2 and rooms of 20 or more agents; no helper strip) is 122 px wide. With the rename pencil (097 FR-357 keeps 20 px of padding at its right end) its content box is **93 px** in the first round (**final, round 5: 99 px**, with 3 px of left padding and an 18 px reserve for the pencil: the numbers of this paragraph and of FR-312 below that say 93, 96 or 20 px are the first rounds', kept as the record). Measured in the simulator (Electron, macOS, 24 agents at their limit, 1280x800, a plate with 3 helpers, the third failed): the word "Limit reached" **71.6 px** (68.4 px with AppKit's font; "Coffee break" is not drawn on a plate, it is the away ticket), the `+3` **13.4 px**, one helper dot **9 px**, the plate's own status dot **10.9 px**, a "!" **3.8 px**, and the line's gap 4 px. Word, `+N`, the plate's own dot and one helper dot need 71.6 + 13.4 + 10.9 + 9 = 105 px (plus gaps), 12 px more than the 93 px: no width choice of the existing parts fits them, and #1564's layout gave the word 61 px ("Limit reac...") and the dot nothing. Widening the plate is not possible here: the plate is the cabinet's width (the art's 44 grid pixels at g = 2) and the gap between cabinets is 2 px (`AISLE_GAP`), so a wider plate would overlap its neighbour; the pencil's 20 px are 097 FR-357's rule; shortening the word is not an option (the status words are the same everywhere).

- **FR-312** (102's own number) **Design choice (the Linux lane's and the agent's, not an owner decision): on the narrow cabinet's plate with its pencil and helpers, the plate's own status dot is not drawn and a marked count says what the dots cannot.** (1) The status word is whole: with the own dot gone the word (71.6 px) and the `+3!` (16.8 px; 17.2 px as a box) need 88.8 px; the first round of this change left 1 px of the 93 (92 of 93), which Linux's Noto Sans (word 72.2 px, `+3` 12 px, `!` 2.8 px: 1.0 px spare at DPR 1 and 2) showed to be too thin for Windows' wider Segoe UI, so the plate's left padding is 4 px instead of 7 and the 1 px gap between the `+N` and the word is gone (only on this plate, with a pencil): the content box was **96 px** and the word's room, measured as the content box's right end less the `+3!` less the word, **7.6 px** on macOS/Electron (11.3 px with the plain `+2`). **[Superseded by round 5 and round 8 below: the final geometry is a left padding of 3 px, a pencil reserve of 18 px, a content box of 99 px asserted by the driver (+-0.5), regular weight; the room is 13.84 px with a failed helper and a pencil (17.45 plain).]** The driver asserts at least **3 px** (`NARROW_ROOM`), so a font up to 3 px (4 %) wider than this one, or a regression below that, fails with the numbers; past that the word is cut by an ellipsis again, never the `+N!`. (The task run has no gap of its own on this plate, so it takes none of the word's room.) The state is still said by the word, the plate's border and fill (limit and waiting), the marquee and the cue. (2) The count is **marked**: `+N!` when at least one helper failed, waits or has reached its limit (counted by the model, `helpUrgent`, with the same predicate that orders the heads and the dots: `prio` in `app.js` (001 FR-089), which reads the RAW state, `waiting` or `error`; **a helper whose light is `limit` counts**: its raw state is `waiting` (the main-process state machine sets `a.limit` together with `set('waiting', ...)`), `prio` puts it first, `dotClass` draws it as a waiting dot, and the mark and the name say it as they say a failed one. Round 5 wrongly dropped `limit` from a second, hand-written list in `realoffice.js`; round 6 removes that list. *Can a helper really be at its limit?* Not through the real events: the renderer hands a limit event to the session, never to a helper (`if(e.agentId&&e.type!=='limit')return onSubEvent`, and `agentstate.ts` says "limit is never a helper's"), so the case is defensive; the driver sets it to pin the wiring), plain `+N` when none did; the "!" exists only on this plate (`.ro-hm`, created only where the plate is the narrow one, `cab-narrow`, and drawn on every narrow plate with helpers, with or without a pencil (FR-313), `aria-hidden`), and **only this plate's** accessible name and tooltip say it in words (". 3 helpers, 1 failed or waiting"; `helpWordsOf`), so the count is not a glyph alone. Every other fallback plate in every world keeps FR-308/FR-311's name (". 6 helpers."), which `check-097-helpers` asserts. (3) The helpers' dots stay in the plate's markup in the heads' order (waiting and error first; FR-311) and show whenever the line has room (they do not here: 0 of 3 shown on this plate, as the record of FR-311 said: a dot needs 11 px and the line has 7.6 px over); a failed or waiting helper's dot is never dropped before an OK helper's. (4) The `+N` is whole. Every other plate keeps FR-311 (the word, the `+N`, then the dots). **The gate is the arcade, not a width:** the mark, the name's words and the dropped dot apply only where the Retro world is drawn on a cabinet layout (`ARC` in `realoffice.js`), and only to a plate 122.5 px wide or less there. The default and the cave Real office draw strip-less fallback plates of that width too (reproduced in the simulator: 800x600, 20 agents, `simhelpers=3`: the narrowest plate is 86 px; at 1280x800 the narrowest is 133 px, so no such plate there): their plates keep FR-308's name ". N helpers. Open chat panel." and draw no mark. The first version of this change gated on the width alone and would have changed those plates' names in every world (found by the second read).
- Why not the other choices: *the pencil yields its 20 px to the word* would give 113 px for 105 px of parts but leaves the pencil over the word's end (FR-357: it covers nothing) and the test needs a plate WITH a pencil; *the dot replaces the +N when one helper failed* drops the count, which says how many helpers there are (FR-308); *a marked count alone* does not fit while the own dot (10.9 px) stays: 13.4 + 3.8 + 10.9 + 71.6 = 99.7 px.
- **Room and spare, side by side (second read).** *Spare* (the first round, and the Linux lane's 1.0 px): the content box less the two boxes of the line, `content - (l1 box + l2 box)`; l1's box is wider than the `+N!` text by what it absorbs of the free width (its shrink factor is huge), so the spare counts room the word cannot use. *Room* (this round's `NARROW_ROOM`, in the driver's `rec.room`): `content box right - the +N!'s right edge - what the word needs in its own line` (the word's width and whatever the plate draws before it in that line: an own dot and its gap; the first version of this measure left the own dot out and was blind to it, which the third round of #1599 corrected); with the line's ellipsis in the no-helper plates (`... - the width of "…"`). They are different quantities: on the first geometry the room, computed from the same numbers, is 93 - 16.8 - 71.6 - 1 (the 1 px gap) = 3.6 px, not 1.0; this round frees 3 px of padding and the 1 px gap (3.6 + 4 = 7.6, measured 7.64). Room is the one asserted.
- **Margin and fonts** (first rounds; superseded by rounds 5 and 8: content box 99 px, room 13.84 px). Measured (macOS/Electron, DPR 1): content box 96 px; room 7.6 px with a failed helper, 11.3 px without; Linux/Xvfb (Noto Sans) measured by the Linux lane on the first geometry: word 72.2 px (0.6 px wider than here), spare 1.0 px at DPR 1 and 2, so the same geometry gives about 7.0 px there; AppKit's font is 3.2 px narrower than this one. Windows (Segoe UI) not measured: `[KNOWN GAP]`.
- `test:` `shots-102-arcade` (*issue 1574*), at 1280x800, 24 agents at their limit with 3 helpers (the third failed) and with 2 (none failed): the narrow plate with a pencil, helpers and "Limit reached" must be drawn (not a vacuous pass: the check names `cab-narrow`, `has-ren`, `has-help`, width 122); on it the word's Range lies inside the plate's content box **exactly** (0.02 px, a layout unit is 1/64 px) and is not ellipsised (no allowed ellipsis any more), the `+N` is whole, the word keeps at least 3 px of room (`NARROW_ROOM`; 7.6 px measured), with a failed helper the count reads `+3!` with the mark whole in view, with none it has no mark, the dots' order is urgent before OK, and the plate's name says ". 3 helpers, 1 failed or waiting." (". 2 helpers." with none); and at 15 agents, 1280x800 (the 146 px plates with a strip-less fallback and a failed helper) the name keeps FR-311's ". 3 helpers." with no words for the failed one. The failure messages print the numbers. Mutants (`ARCADE_MUTANT`), each killed at its own label: `narrowdot` (the own dot drawn again) *the status word "Limit reached" is whole*; `wordpx` (the word wider than its box by 0.05 px, found by bisection) *the status word "Limit reached" is whole* (a cut `scrollWidth`, an integer, would have passed); `thin` (the word whole but with 2 px of room) *the word keeps at least 3 px of room*; `nomark` *a failed helper is marked*; `markalways` *no failed or waiting helper: no mark*; `narrowclip` *a failed or waiting helper's dot comes before every OK helper's*; `namescope` (the extra name words on every plate) *a plate that is not the narrow one keeps its name "N helpers"*; `worldgate` (the arcade's rules on every plate that narrow: the gate removed) *a default or cave plate that is as narrow as the arcade's keeps FR-308's name* (the case draws the default and the cave office at 800x600 with 20 agents: the narrowest plates with a failed helper are 86 px; non-vacuous: at least one must be drawn); `nopenmark` and `nopendot` (below). Pictures: [`102-arcade-narrow-plate-1280x800.png`](../../docs/screenshots/102-arcade-narrow-plate-1280x800.png) (every narrow plate with helpers reads "+3! Limit reached", 271 KB).
- **Answers recorded from the simulator (second read of #1599).** (a) *Exactness at a thin margin:* the old whole-word check compared `scrollWidth` with `clientWidth`, both rounded to integers, so a cut under half a pixel passed; the check now compares the word text's Range with the content box to 0.02 px, and the `wordpx` mutant (0.05 px) dies at it. (b) *Does the Range check see an ellipsis?* No: a Range reports the unclipped text, and the plate's line ends in an ellipsis that takes about 9.6 px (the measured width of "…") of the last letters. Probe, a plate with a pencil and no helpers with the own dot drawn (mutant `nohelpdot`, below): the word's Range ended 9.5 px inside the content box, yet the plate read "Limit reache…" (the picture's Omar and Priya): the check passed while the plate was cut. The check now adds the ellipsis's width to the word's right end when the line runs on past the word. (c) *A narrow plate with helpers and no pencil* (a session `canRenameSession` refuses: it needs `live && !parent && info && bridge.renameSession`, so in practice a finished or stopped parent with a failed helper; probe: the class `has-ren` removed on the narrow plates): content box 106 px; the line was the `+3` (16.4 px), the own dot (10.9 px) and the word (71.6 px), 105 of 106: the word and the `+N` whole, but 0 of 3 dots and no mark, while the plate's name (JS) already said ", 1 failed or waiting": the screen and the name disagreed. **Fixed in FR-313's extension below.** (d) *`limit` counts as failed or waiting:* intended, stated in FR-312 (2).
- 097 FR-016's "status icon and word" on the plate: on the arcade's narrow cabinet plate with a pencil the plate's own icon is not drawn (the word, the border and the marquee carry the state): this FR-312.
- `[KNOWN GAP]` In that picture the narrow plates with a pencil and **no helpers** (Omar and Priya in the limit state; Agent16 has helpers and reads "+3! Limit reached") still read "Limit reache...": the plate's own dot and the word do not leave room for the line's ellipsis. Issue #1600; outside this round's first commit (see the next section if it is folded in). macOS/Electron only; Windows and Linux (Segoe UI, other fonts) not run here; Linux/Xvfb (Noto Sans) passed the first geometry with 1.0 px spare.

### 2026-11-12: the narrow plate with a pencil and no helpers (issue #1600; folded into #1574's PR; the plate's own dot)

On the narrow cabinet's plate with a pencil and **no helpers** (Omar and Priya at their limit in the picture of the section above) the line is the plate's own status dot (10.9 px), the word (71.6 px) and the task, which runs on and ends in an ellipsis of 9.59 px (the width of "…" in the plate's font). With the dot drawn, measured on the first geometry (content box 93 px), the word's Range ended 9.5 px inside the box: it fits, but the ellipsis then takes the word's last letter, so the line read "Limit reache..."; the earlier Range check passed it (answer (b) above).

- **FR-313** (102's own number) **The narrow cabinet's plate does not draw its own status dot, and marks the count, whenever it has a pencil or helpers.** FR-312's rule without its `has-ren` and `has-help` conjunction: `.cab-narrow` plates with a pencil **or** helpers drop the own dot, **in every state** (working, thinking, waiting, limit, error, done, idle, asleep: the state is still said by the word, the plate's border and fill, the marquee and the cue, as 097 FR-046 asks: never colour or a dot alone), and `.cab-narrow` plates with helpers draw the `+N!` mark, **with or without a pencil**; a plate with neither pencil nor helpers keeps its dot, so the word is whole beside the line's ellipsis: measured room (the content box's right end less the word less the ellipsis) **14.8 px** on macOS/Electron, asserted at least 3 px (`NARROW_ROOM`). Other plates are unchanged.
- `test:` `shots-102-arcade` (*issue 1600*), at 1280x800, 24 agents at their limit and **no helpers** (`simhelpers=0`): a plate with `cab-narrow`, `has-ren`, no helpers and "Limit reached" must be drawn (not vacuous), its word whole (the exact Range check, the ellipsis of the running line taken in) and 3 px of room. Mutant `nohelpdot` (the own dot drawn again on the no-helper narrow plate; applied only in that scene) is killed **by construction** at the label *the plate's own status dot is not drawn* (asserted directly on the dot's box, `ownDot`, so no font can hide it); its room is the second guard, and it is not at *is whole* either: with the dot back the word is still whole (the exact check passes) but its room, measured with the ellipsis taken in, is **2.93 px** against the written 3 px (14.8 px without the dot); the room label is `issue 1600: 1280x800 narrow limit no helpers: on the narrow plate with no helpers the word keeps at least 3 px of room (the ellipsis of the line taken in): [2.928,...]`. The margin between the mutant (2.93) and the threshold (3) is thin by chance of this font, which is why the direct assert exists; the dot costs 11.9 px. In the helped narrow scene the same plates (Omar and Priya) are also checked by the generic plate check (the word whole on every plate, FR-311's label), which is red between the two commits of this change and green with FR-313.
- The picture of the section above is retaken after FR-313: the narrow plates without helpers read "Limit reached" whole too. `[KNOWN GAP]` resolved: the "Omar and Priya" gap above.
- **The extension (round 2 of #1599): helpers and no pencil.** `test:` the same driver, 24 agents at their limit with 3 helpers, the pencil removed from the narrow plates (class and button, as the app draws a session it cannot rename; the simulator's sessions all can): the plate must be drawn (a `cab-narrow` plate with helpers, no pencil, "Limit reached"), its word whole, its own dot not drawn, the count `+3!` with the mark whole in view, the name ". 3 helpers, 1 failed or waiting.", and the word's room at least 3 px. Measured (macOS/Electron): content box **106 px**, room **17.6 px**, the line's boxes 29.4 (+N and the dots' row) + 75.6. Mutants killed at their own labels: `nopenmark` (the mark not drawn without a pencil) *a failed helper is marked* (tag `no pencil`), `nopendot` (a 1 px own dot without a pencil) *the plate's own status dot is not drawn*, and `nopendotfull` (the own dot at its real size, 9 px, with 7 px of margin: the word stays whole, the room falls to 1.6 px from 17.6) *the word keeps at least 3 px of room*, so both labels are pinned.
- **Design note, decided (rounds 3 and 4 of #1599): a helper's mark never touches the word.** Picture [`102-arcade-narrow-working-zoom-1280x800.png`](../../docs/screenshots/102-arcade-narrow-working-zoom-1280x800.png) (a 5x zoom of a Working plate with 3 helpers, the third failed, at 1280x800; retaken in round 4 and read). *The problem (third round):* with the plate's own dot gone the plate read `+3!  ▲ ● ●Working`: the helpers' marks sat where the own dot used to be, with nothing between the last mark and the word, so a green "done" mark before "Working" read as a contradiction of the plate's state and a red triangle there would have read as an error on the plate itself. *The design choice (Linux lane, round 4; not an owner decision):* the last helper mark shown is **4 px** from the word, and the marks are 2 px apart as before; the state is carried by the word, the plate's border and fill, the marquee and the cue (FR-313's rule), and the helpers' marks are small marks (9 px) that belong to the count. *Why 4 px and why on the marks:* a word space in this font is about 3.2 px, so 3 px would read as a space inside the cluster and 4 px is a break; twice the 2 px between the marks. The 4 px is the margin after each mark (the marks after the first pull back by 4 px, so they stay 2 px apart), not a gap on the plate: a mark that wraps out of sight takes its margin with it, so a plate that shows no mark keeps its room (**measured unchanged: "Limit reached" with a failed helper and a pencil 7.64 px, plain `+2` 11.25, helpers and no pencil 17.64, no helpers 14.8**), and 0 of 3 marks still show there. *Measured:* on the Working and Waiting plates with three marks shown the gap between the last mark's right edge and the word's left edge is **4.000 px**; a Thinking plate that shows two marks has 14.1 px (the third did not fit; the slack of the line is left before the word). The `+3!` still leads and the failed helper's triangle is the first mark.
- `test:` `shots-102-arcade`, the Working case (1280x800, 24 agents, `simhelpers=3`): a narrow plate with helpers, the word "Working" and at least one mark shown must be drawn (not vacuous), the failed helper's mark among those shown, and the gap from the last mark shown to the word's Range at least 4 px (to 0.02 px). Mutant `nogap` (the marks' margin removed) fails *the last helper mark shown is at least 4 px from the word*; with it the measured gap is 0.

*The failure:* CI's Linux font (DejaVu Sans as `system-ui`) measures "Limit reached" at **76 px** (bold, the 600 weight a limit or waiting plate has): on the first rounds' 96 px content box the word ended 5 px past the box (`+3!Limit reac...`). The 3 px floor had been tuned to macOS (word 71.6 px bold) and Noto Sans (72.2 px), two narrow fonts; Noto is not CI's font. Windows (Segoe UI) was never measured.

The content box is 99 px (was 96 px). *Abbreviating* "Limit reached" (a different word on one plate; the status words are the same everywhere) is not done: the geometry holds without it.
- **Round 8 (review of #1599): the geometry is asserted, not only the room.** The room floor of 3 px let a revert of the padding to 4 px left and 20 px right pass (in the system font and in Verdana the old geometry leaves more than 3 px of room). `shots-102-arcade` now asserts, on the narrow plate with a pencil, the **content box 99 px (+-0.5)** and the **computed padding 3 px left and 18 px right** (layout, not font): the mutant `oldpad` (4 px / 20 px) dies at that label in both entries. It also asserts that the word's end (and its ellipsis) and the content box end **no later than the pencil icon's left edge** (`penClear` >= 0, `penGap` >= 0; the icon's rect is read from the button's child): measured on macOS **1.5 px** clear (the word fills the content box, so both are 1.5 px); the mutant `penclose` (the pencil 2 px nearer: `left: anchor(right) - 26px`) dies at that label (clear -0.5). The FR-259 pose scene was changed in round 8 to wait for 5 people at cabinets; that was wrong, see round 9 below.
- **Measured, room = content right - the `+N!`'s right edge - what the word needs (spec above), macOS/Electron:** with the system font (word 68.4 px regular): failed helper and pencil **13.84 px**, plain `+2` 17.45, helpers and no pencil 20.84, no helpers 22.05. With **Verdana** forced on the plates (`ARCADE_FONT=wide`; word 71.6 px regular, 80.5 px bold): 7.97, 12.11, 14.97, 18.80. The **old geometry** (600 weight, 7 px padding left, 20 px right; mutant `oldgeometry`) in Verdana: word 80.5 px, mark 19.4 px, room **-6.95 px**: cut. DejaVu Sans is not installed on this Mac: the Linux CI font's regular weight is not measured here, estimated about 69 to 70 px for the word and 25 px for the count by the Linux lane's numbers, which gives about 4 to 5 px of room; Linux CI is the check. `[KNOWN GAP]` Windows (Segoe UI) not measured; its UI shards run this driver, and Verdana (the wide stand-in) is wider than Segoe UI.

### 2026-11-12: round 3 of issue 1558 (two red checks on CI: the lounge plate's two runs, and the narrow plate in a wider font; 102 FR-311)

- **The word and the task are one run where the line is a `-webkit-box`.** `plateFill` writes `.ro-wd` and `.ro-tk` on every plate of every world. The lounge, waiting, limit and asleep plates of Photo office and the cave clamp their line to two lines with `display:-webkit-box` (`realoffice.css`); a child of that box is a box of its own, so the two spans overlapped (World matrix, `lounge` at 1280x800 in `photo` and `cave`: `overlap span.ro-wd x span.ro-tk by 37.1x12.0px` measured on macOS, 38.5x12.0 on Linux CI). Decided by measurement: `display:inline` on the spans does not help (a child of a flex or `-webkit-box` container is blockified whatever its `display`), so those plates give the spans `display:contents` (`realoffice.css`): the two texts are again one run of text in the box, as the single text node they replaced, and the clamp still gives two lines (the line is 26 px, 2 x 13.125, measured, in `photo`, `cave` and `retro`). The arcade's plate with helpers sets `display:inline` on the spans in `world.css` (a flex item is blockified, so they stay the flex items FR-311 describes, and a mutant that makes the line a block shows them inline as before), so FR-311's layout is unchanged. One DOM for every world was kept (the other option, a single text node outside the arcade's plate with helpers, would make `plateFill` read the world and the helpers, and the text node must be swapped when a helper arrives). `test:` the World matrix itself (it fails the overlap at `lounge / photo` and `lounge / cave` at 1280x800 with the old CSS, passes 28 of 28 at both sizes with this one; the old CSS is the mutant, killed at that label).
- **The narrow plate's word may be cut by an ellipsis in a font wider than macOS's; the check says so exactly.** The KNOWN GAP above is font-dependent. Measured on macOS (Verdana in place of the system font, `ARCADE_FONT=wide`): "Limit reached" with the dot fits the plate's 93 px content box by under 1 px; with 0.1 px of letter spacing (1.3 px wider) it ends 2 px past the box and the line shows "Limit reac..." (Linux CI, with its fallback font, ended 1 px past: the word box 84 px). The driver's exemption for the ellipsis (`ell`, this plate only) tested the *span's* box, which is not clipped on a plate without helpers, so a correctly ellipsised word failed by 1 px. It now asks what the user sees: the box that clips the word (`.ro-wd` where the plate has helpers, else the line) has `text-overflow:ellipsis`, `overflow-x:hidden`, is cut (`scrollWidth > clientWidth`) and ends inside the content box. A bare cut, or a word that runs past its box under the pencil, still fails. The whole-word fix on this plate (regular weight, 3 px less padding, a 18 px pencil reserve: about 9 px more room in Verdana) is the work of the plate of issue 1574 (FR-312), not repeated here, so the two do not fight over the same rules.
- `test:` (as amended by the merge with the plate of issue 1574, FR-312) the ellipsis path is exercised by the case *narrow squeezed* of `shots-102-arcade`, on any machine: the word made 8 px wider than the room its plate gives it (letter spacing computed per plate from the measured room; it was a fixed 1.4 px until round 9), which must then be **cut** (the case asserts its clipping box overflows, `scrollWidth > clientWidth`, so a change that makes the word fit cannot pass it untested; the test does not depend on the ellipsis, so `noell` and `spill` still reach the next assert) and end in an ellipsis inside its own box, the `+N` whole. Mutants `noell` (the word cut with no ellipsis) and `spill` (the word runs past its box) fail at "issue 1558: ... narrow squeezed: where the word does not fit it ends in an ellipsis inside its own box ..."; `squeezefit` (the word made to fit) fails at "... narrow squeezed: the word is cut ...". `ARCADE_FONT=wide` (test only) is now the mode of the `shots-102-arcade wide font` entry, which runs the narrow cases alone in Verdana, else DejaVu Sans, with no letter spacing (it was: every `markCheck` scene of the driver with 0.1 px of spacing, and noell/spill failing at the narrow limit label, before the merge). `[KNOWN GAP]` Windows and Linux not run by the author; the Linux font was not named in the CI log.

### 2026-11-12: the merge with #1564's round 3 (round 6 of #1599)

- #1564's round 3 (spec 102 FR-311) tested the narrow plate's word *cut by an ellipsis* in a wider font and gave `ARCADE_FONT=wide` (Verdana, else DejaVu Sans, 0.1 px of letter spacing, on every `markCheck` scene) and the mutants `noell` and `spill`. This branch's FR-312 round 5 makes the word whole in Verdana and DejaVu (regular weight, wider box), so **that allowance is no longer used by a real font on the narrow plate**: the merged driver keeps its `ell` parameter and its clipping-box test (`.ro-wd` where the plate has helpers, else the line: an ellipsis of the box that clips, inside the content box), and keeps `noell` and `spill`, and exercises them on purpose: the case *narrow squeezed* makes the word 8 px wider than the room its plate gives it (by letter spacing computed per plate: a fixed 1.4 px of the earlier rounds cut it on macOS, Verdana and DejaVu but not in Windows' font) and asserts the word then ends in an ellipsis inside its own box and the `+N` stays whole; `noell` (a clip) and `spill` (overflow visible) die at its label. The wide-font mode of this branch (narrow cases alone, the font-load check, no letter spacing) is the one kept; #1564's 0.1 px spacing is dropped (it made the old geometry's word overflow by under 1 px; the new geometry needs no help).


## Changes 2026-11-11: a strip over the floor does not make the arcade scroll at 1280x800 (T308, issue #1566, no new FR)

[FR-229](spec.md) and FR-293 (15 agents fit 1440x900 and 1280x800, no office scroll) hold with a notice strip over the floor. **Cause (measured in the simulator, 15 agents, `simkinds=mixed`, Retro arcade, 1280x800):** the MCP strip (one line, 31 px) leaves the office 666 px (the floor's 690 px less its 12 px padding top and bottom). The arcade's smallest hall for 15 agents (3 rows of 186 px, a 96 px lobby, the 20 px lane, 8 px of margin) is 682 px, so no plan fitted, `aislesLayout` took its scroll fallback (700 px) and `#floor` scrolled (scrollHeight 724 against 690, the bottom row cut off). The Photo office fits the same 666 px, so it never scrolled. Nothing was left stuck by the strip: after Dismiss the arcade fits again (697 of 721 px), and at 1440x900 it fits with and without the strip. The earlier note (FR-256, the bend: 3 px to spare under a banner) no longer held after the 21 px plate (T276). **Change:** when a Real office whose world uses `aisles` would scroll, the renderer asks the layout once more with the floor's own vertical padding added (`opts.lend`, 24 px; only the 12 px at the bottom when a filter note sits in the top padding); if that fits it is used and `#floor` drops that padding (`#floor.ro-tight`, which drops only the floor's top and bottom padding (the sides stay 16 px), set from the layout on every draw, removed at the start of the next, so a closed strip gives the padding back). A hall that cannot fit even then (more than 15 agents) scrolls as before. `sim:` `shots-058-mcp-launch` (Photo office) and `shots-058-mcp-launch retro` (the arcade) at both sizes: no office scroll with the strip shown and after Dismiss, `ro-tight` gone once it closes; the arcade case fails with `lend` set to 0. **No loop:** the room is always measured with the class off (it is removed at the start of every draw and set again in the same task), so the lend decision cannot flip between a 666 px and a 690 px measurement when the floor's size changes under the resize observer; the driver reads the office five times 100 ms apart while the strip shows and they must be equal. **Decided 2026-11-11 by the coordinator under the calm-UI rule (15 agents fit 1440x900 and 1280x800, no overlap or office scroll):** the hall may sit flush with the strip and the window's bottom edge (plus the layout's own 8 px) while a strip shows; a scrolling office with a cut bottom row is the defect, a missing 12 px margin is not. **Owner decision (Vikram, 2026-11-12 12:51 UTC, recorded as a comment on #1573):** (1) at 1280x800 with the MCP strip shown, the hall's pink top frame and dark bottom frame being cut off (the missing 12 px margin) is accepted; (2) two strips at once or the open MCP list still scrolling under the arcade is accepted for now, tracked as #1589, until #1573 lands and we decide what yields. `[KNOWN GAP]` with a status filter set (a filter note takes 40 px of top padding) 11 to 15 agents still scroll at 1280x800 (room 669 plus 12 lent is 681 against 682; pre-existing, not changed here). `[KNOWN GAP]` two strips at once (MCP and crash: 659 + 24 = 683, one px to spare on macOS), a strip a pixel taller on Linux or Windows, and the MCP list open (up to 210 px) still scroll (two strips and the open list: accepted for now by the owner decision above, issue #1589); Photo has no such rule. `[KNOWN GAP]` `scripts/test-realoffice-aisles.ts` `SIZES` (1440x848, 1280x748) are larger than the measured rooms (797 and 697 px); with the measured ones a folder of 4 or fewer is split in that test (pre-existing, hidden by the larger sizes): the threshold this fix depends on is pinned there at the measured heights (666 scrolls, 682 and 690 fit); the rest of `SIZES` is not changed. **Round 3 (pre-review of 2026-11-12) adds tests only, no behaviour change:** three more entries of `shots-058-mcp-launch` (each fails with its own label when its rule is dropped): `retro 16` (16 agents at 1280x800 with the strip: the lent layout still scrolls, so `ro-tight` stays off and the office scrolls as before; kills `if (!l2.scroll)` made `if (true)`), `retro filter` (a status filter showing 11 to 15 agents with the strip: `ro-tight` is never set while the floor still scrolls, and the filter note keeps its top padding; kills the lend taking the top padding under a note) and `photo 18` (18 agents at 1280x760, where Photo scrolls and the lend would rescue it: a world whose arrangement is not `aisles` is never lent the padding; kills the `meta.arrangement === 'aisles'` guard dropped). **Task number:** `specs:numbers` reports T308 as also in the stacked PRs for issue 1590 and issue 1588: both branches are built on this one and carry its T308 line unchanged, and add their own T309 and T310; it is a stack, not a second T308, and nothing is renumbered unless a different head takes T308 before this lands (recheck at merge). Other strips (restore, away, crash, follow) get the same rule but were not run; macOS only, not run on Linux or Windows.

### 2026-11-12: round 9 of #1599: why the FR-259 scene failed on another machine, and the Windows failure

- **The pose scene (FR-259, `shots-102-arcade`, 15 agents at 1440x900) was racing the simulator.** A review's Linux box failed it with `people at cabinets: 4` and, with round 8's 15 s wait, stayed at 4 for the whole 15 s: not a timing wait that never ends, but a state that settles. Measured on the Mac, 20 loads idle and 20 more under four CPU-bound `yes` loops (load average 13), each read once at the end of the load and once 2.5 s later: **n = 9 at the load in 40 of 40 runs, n = 4 at 2.5 s in 40 of 40**, idle and loaded alike (the same six agents shown away or absent every time). Cause: the simulator is not random here (no `Math.random` in the roster's states or places) but its agents **leave their cabinets within seconds**: the waiting and done ones go to places (the roster's Jonas, Kenji, Lena, Omar, Noor, Maya) and its visitors (Ada, Elif, Sven, Omar, Clara, Rosa) are away at theirs, so the settled count is 4 of 13 desks; a frozen sim with one state for every agent seats 9 of 15 (14 for 1.5 s while the people walk off), 6 with all waiting, 1 of 7 with all done. The old floor of 5 held only while the first draw was fresh, and a machine slower between the load and the read (here only milliseconds, there seconds) read the settled 4.
- **Fix: the scene sets what it needs.** The sim is frozen (`simfreeze`), the agents get one of six states in turn (working, thinking, error, limit, idle, asleep: the states that keep a person at the cabinet), and the scene waits, with a labelled 15 s timeout, until the layout shows **those states** (an agent that carries the limit flag, the sim's Omar, shows the limit light whatever is set and is left out of the comparison) **and exactly 9 people at cabinets** (15 agents less the 6 visitors away at their places), each drawn with its pose by state (FR-259's table). Measured: reached after **668 to 729 ms** in 40 of 40 runs, idle and under load. It then reloads the sim running for the scenes below, as before. What it proves: the pose of every person who stands at a cabinet, for six states, with an exact count (not a floor). Mutant `poseempty` (every agent `done`: they go to the lounge, n is 1, not 9) fails at *FR-259: within 15 s exactly 9 people stand at cabinets ...* (line 380).
- **The Windows failure (`UI shard (windows-latest 3/4)` on e66c61de8, run 43268603074, job 131358911697) is NOT the n:4 count.** The log: `FAIL shots-102-arcade 20 s DRIVER FAILED AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: issue 1558: 1440x900 narrow squeezed: the word is cut (its clipping box overflows), or this case would not test the ellipsis at all: [false,false,false,false,...` (24 values, all false), and again on the suite's one retry (`FAIL again shots-102-arcade`). It is the *squeezed* case of the merge (round 7): it added a fixed 1.4 px of letter spacing (about 18 px) and asserted the word was then cut; in Windows' font the word, narrower than in macOS, Verdana or DejaVu, plus 18 px still fitted its room, so nothing was cut and the case (correctly) refused to pass untested. **Inferred, not measured on Windows** (the log prints only the `cut` flags). The squeezed case now makes the word 8 px wider than the room its plate gives it, computed per plate, so it cuts in any font. The round 8 asserts (content box 99 px +-0.5, padding 3 and 18 px, the pencil's clear gap) were not reached on Windows: the squeezed case runs first in the narrow cases; whether they hold in Segoe UI is open until the next Windows run (the content box and padding are layout; the pencil clear gap is the content box's end to the icon, which does not depend on the word's width).


## Changes 2026-11-12: `test-realoffice-aisles` runs at the measured rooms, and small folders stay whole in a 682 to 713 px room, up to 815 px with helpers (T309, issue #1590, no new FR; a product change)

The test's `SIZES` were the floors 1440x848 and 1280x748, taller than the rooms the app measures (797 px at 1440x900 and 697 px at 1280x800: the floor less its 12 px padding top and bottom, see 2026-11-11, T308). That is how the 15-agent strip case stayed green while the app scrolled. `SIZES` are now those rooms, taken from one constant the 15-agent block shares. **Cause of the failure that showed (measured with `aislesLayout`, Retro arcade, width 1280):** 10 agents in 3 folders (4, 3, 3) or 4 folders keep every folder whole only in three rows (4 | 3 | 3), which at g = 2 and the narrowest cabinet need 558 px of floor. `planAt` tried the lower lobby only when the plan at the set lobby (`AISLE_LOBBY`, 128 px) did not fit at all, never when it fitted only by cutting a folder across two rows (`keep: false`): at 697 px the hall took two rows of five and split a folder. `lobbyRange`'s 116 px floor already counts the 20 px lane (`lobbyMin` = 116 - 20 = 96), while the loop for 4 agents or fewer in `planAt` treats 116 as the lobby itself; the lowest lobby for these halls is the 96 px of `lobbyMin`, so they need 558 + 96 + 20 + 8 = **682 px**, which 697 has (an earlier note here said 702 and counted the lane twice; it was wrong).

**Changed (two rules, one aim: a folder of 4 or fewer is one group, spec 102 *amended 2026-11-03*):** (1) `planAt` also takes the lowest-lobby plan when the set-lobby plan does not keep every folder of 4 or fewer whole and the lowest-lobby plan does. (2) The helper strip under every plate (FR-306) is dropped when it is what splits such a folder (found reading the simulator picture: with a helper each, 10 agents in 3 folders still split at 697); the plate then keeps +N and dots, as when the hall does not fit with the strip. **Measured** (layout sweep, one pixel steps of room height 560 to 820, 1 to 16 agents in 1 to 8 folders, with and without one helper each): scroll never changes; 10 agents in 3 or 4 folders, 9 in 3 and 10 in 5 to 7 are whole from 682 px (split at 681); without helpers the rule reaches rooms of 682 to 713 px only and the lobby comes down to 96 px, with helpers it reaches 815 px (the highest room the sweep changed; it includes the 1440x900 room of 797 px, where the old plan split 10 agents in 3 folders under their strips). Layouts changed at floor width 1248 (a 1280 px window less 32 px): 996 (192 without helpers); at floor width 900: 166 (32 without helpers); at floor width 1408: 996. Narrow floors change 6 agents in 3 or 4 folders (floor width 860 to 885) and 8 in 3 folders (floor width 890 to 1100). **Not limited to the measured rooms:** a condition on the room height would leave the 1440x900 split with helpers in place, which is the same defect; without helpers the rule already stops by itself at 714 px. The Retro layout hash (4, 12 and 15 agents in 4 folders at 848 and 748 px, which never take the new path) is unchanged; the 682 and 697 pins in `test-realoffice-aisles` carry the change.

**Pictures read** (simulator, Retro arcade, windows giving rooms of 697, 682 and 690 px; not committed): 9 agents in 3 folders at 1280x697 and 10 in 3 at 1280x682 (lobby 106 and 96 px): every visitor stands whole and every name chip reads in full; the rows are tight (a folder's sign sits directly under the plates above) but nothing overlaps or scrolls. The chip "Coffee corner" is partly covered by "Bank" in a window 900 px wide (floor 868 px) with 8 or 9 agents; that is the lobby's width, it is the same without this change, and it was not made worse. No clamp of the lobby was needed.

**Owner decision (Vikram, 2026-11-12 05:26 UTC, recorded as a comment on #1595): allowed, beyond spec 102 option A** (a separate and earlier decision than the 12:51 UTC one about the strip recorded on #1573 in the T308 paragraph above: that paragraph stands first only because #1573 landed first; this one is the 05:26 UTC time of the Telegram answer, the comment on #1595 is stamped 05:27 UTC) (`:843` and `:570`, and `AISLE_LOBBY_LOW`). The question, and the answer 1:
1. Recommended, and the owner's answer: allow it. When a folder of 4 or fewer would be split across two rows, the arcade lowers the lobby (down to 96 px; 15 agents already get 106 px at 1280x697 through `planAt`'s existing lower-lobby fallback when the 128 px plan does not fit) and, if that is not enough, drops the helper heads under the plates (they show +N); every folder stays whole and nothing scrolls.
2. Do not allow it: keep the 128 px lobby and the helper heads, and a folder of 4 or fewer is split across two rows in a 682 to 713 px room (and 10 agents with helpers at 1440x900).
Facts for the answer: this goes beyond option A, which covers 4 or fewer agents with the lobby down to 116 px (spec `:843`); the rule now reaches 8 to 10 agents (and 6 agents in 3 or 4 folders at floor widths 860 to 885, 8 in 3 folders at 890 to 1100) and a lobby of 96 px. the owner's 2026-11-03 rule that the lobby is no smaller at 1280x800 than at 1440x900 is checked by `test-realoffice-aisles` at 748 and 848 px floors, not at the measured rooms; at the measured rooms 10 agents get a 106 px lobby at 1280x800 and 128 px at 1440x900. With the 31 px notice strip shown (666 px) 10 agents with helpers keep the head strip and split a folder, so the layout changes when the strip is shown or dismissed (a trade-off, not a loop).

The `[KNOWN GAP]` of 2026-11-11 above about `SIZES` is closed by this change. Lobby by room, 10 agents in 3 folders at floor width 1248: 682 px 96, 697 px 106, 713 px 110, 714 px and above 128 (with helpers the same up to 713; 104 at 797). The strip is kept where dropping it would not keep the folders whole: pinned by `test-realoffice-aisles` with 10 agents in 3 or 4 folders, a helper each, at 666 px (strip rows stay 1; the folder splits, the named gap below), which fails when the strip is dropped whatever the plan (round 5 review, rubenlupi). **Remaining gap:** 10 agents in 3 or 4 folders at 1280x800 with the 31 px MCP strip shown (666 px): the folder is split and the hall does not scroll (whole needs 682). No layout-only fix: the #1566 lend (T308) runs only when the plan scrolls, and a split plan does not scroll. `test-realoffice-aisles` names it. Every other count of 1 to 10 agents (1, 3 and 4 folders) at both rooms, with and without the strip, is whole. Not run: Linux, Windows; 13 agents as 4/3/3/3 at floor widths 885 to 905 and 7 agents as 2/3/2 at floor width 900 (named by a second read) did not change in this sweep's models.

## Changes 2026-11-12: the status filter's note does not make the arcade scroll at 1280x800 (T310, issue #1588, no new FR)

[FR-229](spec.md) and FR-293 (15 agents fit 1280x800, no office scroll) hold with a status filter set. **Cause (measured in the simulator, Retro arcade, 1280x800, Working pill set):** the filter note stands in the floor's top padding, which grows from 12 to 40 px (`#floor.has-fnote`), so the office has 669 px (721 less 40 and 12); the hall for 11 to 15 agents needs 682. The #1566 lend (T308) gave back only the bottom 12 px with a note (681: one px short), and the top padding was kept whole. **Decision (measured, not guessed):** lend the top padding too, down to what the note needs. The note is `top: 8px` with a 20 px line, so it ends at 28 px; `#floor.ro-tight.has-fnote` keeps `--fnote-keep: 32px` (4 px of air) and gives up the other 8 px with the bottom 12 px: the hall gets 721 - 32 = 689 px. Moving the note (it would need its own band and a new height for every office) and shrinking the hall by 1 px (the hall is exact at 682, and 1 px less makes the 15-agent block of T308 and T309 disagree) were weighed and left. `renderReal` reads the lent amount from the floor's padding less `--fnote-keep`; the note stays clear of the hall. **Pinned** (`test-realoffice-aisles`, no browser): the kept padding is at least the note's bottom edge (28) and at most 39 (at 39 the hall is 682 and fits, at 40 it is 681 and scrolls); 15, 14 and 11 agents fit at 721 - 32; 15 agents scroll with the padding as the page gives it (669) and with only the bottom lent (681). **Simulator** (`check-102-arcade-filter-note`, Working pill, 1280x800 and 1440x900, 14, 13 and 11 cabinets): the office does not scroll, the note is not covered, `ro-tight` is set at 1280x800 only, and Show all gives the padding back. Mutants: `--fnote-keep: 40px` fails both labels; `20px` fails the clearance label. **Gaps, re-measured on the head that merges T309 (layout probe, width 1248):** (a) (issue #1598) 10 agents in 3 or 4 folders with the note (room 669 px) are still split with no scroll, so the lend does not run (it runs only when the plan scrolls); they are whole from 682, and at 689 (the note's room with the lend) they are whole with a 104 px lobby, so a lend that also ran for a split plan would cover it (not done here; the same cause as the 1280x666 strip case of T309); (b) (nearest open issue #1589, strips stacking: the combination of the MCP strip and the note is to be added there) the MCP strip and the note together (room 638 px, 658 with the lend) still scroll for 11 to 15 agents (the hall needs 682): no fix here; (c) the note's own text is nearly invisible on the arcade's dark floor (contrast 1.09:1, issue #1559; read in the simulator picture of this change and not changed here, so it is not forgotten); (d) `--fnote-keep: 32px` assumes the note is one line. Measured in the simulator at 640, 800 and 1280 px wide with the longest realistic text ("14 hidden (14 need you)", 57 characters) the note is one 20 px line ending at 28 px, and the hall starts at 40 px before this change (no overlap on this branch's base); a wrapped note (larger system text, other fonts) was not reproduced and not measured, so no overlap is claimed or ruled out there. Not run: 15 cabinets in the simulator (its one basic agent never works, so the Working pill shows at most 14; 15 is pinned above), Linux, Windows font metrics.
## Changes 2026-11-12: FR-271 and FR-291 read for the Photo office desk kids; the Flat office keeps its tiles (owner decisions; spec 097 FR-368..FR-376; T235, T253 amended; T326, T327; issues #1643, #1644; no new FR)

**Status: specified, NOT BUILT.** Spec only. The owner asked for helpers as kids playing on the desk (Vikram, Telegram DM, 2026-11-12 18:48 UTC: "can you show sub agents as kids playing on desk?") and decided the design ("Ok with your recommendation on kids and go ahead and generate images"; "We can remove mug if it is useless / ok for all other recommendations, let's make it fun and playful"). The Real office rules are spec 097's dated delta of 2026-11-12 (FR-368..FR-376); this entry records what they change here.

- **FR-271, amended for the Photo office.** In the Photo office the kids stand or sit on the parent's desk top beside the person where there is room (097 FR-368), **as many as fit, at most 6, then `+K`** (FR-271's "three shown per parent at most, then `+N`" stays for the worlds that have not decided otherwise); at about 0.40 of the person's height (097 FR-369); busy desks keep 097's head row with the kid's face (097 FR-370). **Seven poses with props instead of four** (097 FR-371: working, thinking, waiting, error, limit as a defensive pose, done, asleep), from the helper's existing state names. FR-271's "hover card with the helper's task" is **now a tooltip** (owner, Vikram, Telegram DM, 2026-11-12 21:19:57 UTC, 097 Q36 resolved (a): "Ok" = the recommended answer): hovering a kid shows a short tooltip with the helper's task, not the big hover card (#1644).
- **FR-271, the Flat office.** "Kids in Flat and the Photo office first" (T235) changes: **the Flat office keeps its helper tiles** (owner, above). Flat's tile already draws each helper as a small figure in its own scene with its name and status word (spec 001 FR-089..FR-097): that is the feature every world matches under the parity rule (owner 2026-11-04), and Flat's cell of the `helpers` moment is its tiles.
- **FR-291, read for the Photo office.** Clay-toy, visibly a toy, fully dressed, no likeness, never a photorealistic child: unchanged and binding (Q5). Its "they stand beside the parent's plate and nowhere else" was written for the garage and roof worlds and stays there; in the Photo office the kids are on the desk top, never in front of the plate (097 FR-373). The kid art is per world: in the Photo office six kids, each with a pose sheet (097 FR-371), instead of one `helpers.webp` strip.
