- **Changed 2026-11-12 (21:19:57 UTC, owner):** the kid's hover is a short tooltip with the helper's task, not a hover card (097 Q36 resolved (a)); the FR-271 line above is amended in place.
- `[KNOWN GAP]` Nothing here is built; `helpers` is not yet a registered moment in `docs/experience/world-moments.json` (T235, #1644).

**Numbers** (checked 2026-11-12, `npm run specs:numbers`, `origin/main` `c4e1f7ebe`, every remote branch and 49 open PR heads): spec 102 reaches T310 on `main` and T325 on open PR #1599; this entry takes **T326, T327** and no FR.

## Changes 2026-11-13: the World section, first step: picture cards for Plain and the Photo office, with their previews (T266 in part; issue #1400; no new FR)

[FR-300](spec.md), [FR-301](spec.md), [FR-302](spec.md), SC-026. The recorded decisions and the spec are followed as written; this entry records how the work is cut, so each part lands behind the closed flags and nothing of an unreleased world shows.

- **Built in this step (on the previews of T265a; the World choice stays in `localStorage`, as T227 moved no world setting into the settings file: `[KNOWN GAP]`).** Settings > **World** (after Display, before Keyboard): picture cards in one radio group, **Plain** first, then every built-in world whose flag is open (today the Photo office only). A card is the preview, the name, one line and *Built in*; the chosen one has the 2 px `--accent` edge and *In use*; arrows move the focus and Space or Enter choose; the preview zooms by `transform` on hover and focus (about 1.6 times, 220 ms, inside the clip, toward the people), and not at all under Reduced motion or with Motion off. Choosing updates the cards in place (the focused card stays the same element, so an arrow pressed right after Enter is not lost) and every card keeps the room of its *In use* line, so none changes size; *In use* is part of the chosen card's description, so it is announced. Plain is today's flat office and a world card is Real office in that world, so the two Display rows (*Office layout* and *World*) are gone; the line "Real office shows <set> until ..." sits under the cards. **A world behind a closed flag is not listed (FR-301; no "Not open yet" card):** the cave and the arcade appear when their flags open, with a plain tile until their preview is taken.
- **Previews (FR-302).** The cards draw the pictures of T265a (`realoffice/previews/`, `self.DurbarWorldPreviews`, loaded by `index.html`); this step takes none. The previews' script loads on the first draw of the section and, when it arrives, only the cards' pictures are replaced in place (no repaint of the page, so no MCP list reload and the focus, the roving tabindex and the scroll stay). A card whose preview is missing shows a plain tile with its name. `[KNOWN GAP]` the Characters picture cards (T265b's groups, FR-303) are the next step once they merge.
- **Not in this step (stays in T266, T227, T230).** The choice is kept where it was kept (`durbar.officeview` and `durbar.world` in `localStorage`); moving it into `durbar-settings.json` through main and the migration of FR-266 and FR-305 (T227) are not built, and nothing can be migrated before the flip because Retro Pixel is still a theme. The Theme section, Characters as picture cards and the removal of 097's mode button wait for T230 ("with or after"). The catalogue's greyed cards and thumbnails (FR-302) wait for the catalogue.
- **Tests.** `check-settings-worlds` (both sizes, flat and Real office): the rows gone, the section and the group, a closed flag unlisted and an open one listed, a saved cave with its flag closed falling back to the Photo office with no cave card with a tile, one checked card with *In use* and the 2 px accent edge, one Tab stop, arrows move and do not choose, Enter and Space choose and the focus stays, focus outside the card with a gap and not like the choice, 24 px targets and 10.5 px text, the zoom (1.6, transform only, no box moves, clipped), no zoom under Reduced motion or Motion off, forced colours. The drivers that clicked the Office layout buttons or the World row use the cards.

**Numbers:** no new FR or task. **Not verified:** Windows and Linux, a screen reader on the card group, the previews at Compact, a real session.


## Changes 2026-11-13: the arcade's pressed segment button shows its keyboard focus (FR-263, T341, issue #1666, no new FR)

Measured in the simulator (`check-052-focus-ring`, Retro arcade, real pixels from `capturePage`, DPR 1, both sizes, and a picture read; issue #1616 sweep): the pressed segment button (the active Office or List chip, `.topbar .seg button[aria-pressed="true"]`) in the arcade has a focus ring of `outline: 3px solid rgb(17,17,34)` at offset -3px, drawn inside its yellow fill (spec 052 FR-052's rule from #1615: `--brand-mark` inset, written for a chip on a cream strip). In the arcade the strip around the chip is the dark `--surface` of the world's top bar, so the ring reads 15.33:1 against the chip and **1.03:1 against the strip outside it**: in the 4x crop the focused chip is a plain yellow chip, no visible ring.

**Decision.** In the arcade (`html[data-world][data-ui="retro"]`) the pressed segment button's focus ring is **outside the chip**: `outline-color: var(--ink)` (the strip's light ink, rgb(244,238,252), the colour the unpressed segment buttons already ring in) and `outline-offset: 1px`, so a 1px gap of the dark strip separates the ring from the yellow fill. (First tried `--focus`, the world's yellow: it read 15.31:1 but looked like the chip growing a dark line, because ring and fill are the same yellow; the light ring is a different colour from the fill, seen in the picture.) The unfocused pressed chip keeps its yellow fill and dark ink (FR-263), so it still reads as selected, and a focused one adds the ring. Tokens only; the rule lives in `realoffice-world.css` beside FR-263's pressed-chip colour. The unpressed segment buttons (a light inset ring on the dark strip, 15.9:1) and the Flat office, Photo office and user themes are not changed: the Flat retro theme's pressed chip is on a cream strip and reads 16.86:1 and 15.33:1; Photo (ui clean) reads 7.26:1 outside and 6.05:1 inside (both measured, `check-052-focus-ring`, 2026-11-13).

**Test.** `check-052-focus-ring` asserts the arcade look (it printed it only): each bar control, the pressed chip included, is at least 3:1 against the first pixel outside its ring, and the pressed chip's line is labelled `pressed segment`. Mutant `FOCUS_MUTANT=chip` restores the inset `--brand-mark` rule and dies at that label.

- `[KNOWN GAP]` Not verified: Windows, macOS, Linux with a real window manager, DPR 2, a user theme in the arcade (the ring is the world's `--ink`, which themegen pairs with the bar surface at 4.5:1; the driver does not load such a theme in the arcade).

**Numbers** (checked 2026-11-13, `npm run specs:numbers`, `origin/main`, every remote branch and open PR head): this entry takes **T341** and no FR.

## Changes 2026-11-13: a folder of 4 or fewer stays whole under a notice strip at 1280x666 (T342, issue #1598, no new FR)

[FR-229](spec.md) and FR-293 hold with a notice strip over the floor. **Cause (measured in the simulator, Retro arcade, 1280 wide, 10 agents in 3 folders, `simrooms=3`, a 769 px window so the strip leaves the 666 px room of the issue):** the plan fits by cutting a folder of 3 across two rows, so the layout does not scroll and the #1573 lend (`opts.lend`, T308) never ran, as it runs only when the plan scrolls; the hall keeps every folder whole from 682 px. **Change:** `renderReal` also asks the layout again with the lent padding when the first layout cuts a folder of 4 or fewer across rows, and uses it when it does not scroll and cuts none (a scrolling plan is still lent as before; a lent layout that cuts a folder just the same is not used). `sim:` `shots-058-mcp-launch retro` with `DRIVER_CASE=10 DRIVER_AGENTS=10 DRIVER_ROOMS=3 DRIVER_H=769`: before, `r1` is in 2 parts, no scroll, `ro-tight` off; after, every folder is one part, no scroll, `ro-tight` and `lent` set (picture read: three whole folders, lobby low, hall flush with the window's bottom as the 2026-11-11 and 2026-11-12 decisions accept). `test:` `test-realoffice-aisles` pins that the layout at 666 + 24 has no cut folder for 10 agents in 3 and 4 folders. **Not verified:** `simrooms=4` does not reproduce it in the simulator (3, 3, 2, 2 are whole at 666); the status-filter note case (room 669, lend 689, the gap (a) of T310) goes through the same code but was not run in the simulator; 1440x900 is unchanged (the room is 797, the layout does not cut there); Linux and Windows font metrics, macOS.

## Changes 2026-11-13: T265 is split; the character group pictures are made by a pure script (FR-303, T343 = part (b) of T265, issue #1703, no new FR)

[FR-303](spec.md) asks for a group picture per character set, "made by a pure script from the set's own shipped pictures, never new art, at most 64 KB". T265 held that and FR-302's previews in one task; they share no code (one is the simulator and a driver, the other pictures only), so **T265 is split: part (a)** (previews, `simscene=preview`, the driver; issue #1702, named by its own entry) and **part (b)**, this script, issue #1703, **task T343** (#1704's split text names it **T265b**, so that line is ticked and points here: T265b was built as T343, which holds the full record, and there is no second build). T265's own text is part (a)'s to edit.

**What the script does** (`scripts/build-character-groups.py`, Pillow, like `build-kid-pack.py`; `npm run groups:build`): for each set under `src/renderer/realoffice/sets/`, in sorted id order, it cuts a square from the person's `<person>-rest.webp` and lays the squares in rows of a tile of 96 px, the last row centred, on a transparent ground; one WebP per set at `src/renderer/realoffice/groups/<set>.webp` (quality 80, no EXIF, XMP or ICC). Rows: `round(sqrt(n / 1.6))`, columns `ceil(n / rows)`: 15 people are 5 x 3, 13 are 5 x 3 with 3 on the last row, 6 are 3 x 2. No randomness, no network, no AI credits: the same pictures give the same bytes. **Choices made here, since the spec says "head-and-shoulders crops" and no more** (each is one constant in the script): (1) the square is the whole picture for the sets that draw a bust (Cheeky, Minimalist, Monster, Pirate, Prehistoric, Rebels, Royal), because 097's `headCrop` (the face's square, `HEAD_FACE` 0.8) and a shoulders-sized cut of 0.55 of the body both cut hats, crowns and hair in a look at the pictures; (2) for the Office people, whose seated picture shows the body to the chair, the square is 0.55 of `bodyH` from the top of the head (`HS_U`); (3) the pixel set (Retro) is cut the same way, zoomed by one whole number for the whole set with the nearest pixel and saved lossless, so its people keep their size against each other. `set.json` keeps its numbers in the source sheet's pixels, so the script scales them by the shipped picture's height (the Office pictures are resized) and refuses a picture whose proportions differ.

**The budget (FR-303).** Each picture is at most 64 KB: the script lowers the WebP quality (80, 70, 60, 50, 40), then the tile (never under 96 today, so only the quality moves), and fails; it never drops a person (so "13 shown" of the mock is all 13 of Cheeky, and the budget has room). **Measured** (this script, Pillow 10.2): Office people 15 at 50.6 KB, Cheeky 13 at 54.0, Retro 15 lossless at 28.6, Prehistoric 6 at 27.0, Rebels 28.1, Pirate 27.7, Monster 27.0, Royal 26.0, Minimalist 15.5; the mock's 48.6, 53.5 and 48.6 KB were for a different crop. FR-303's face floor (no face under 24 px on a card): the script checks the app's own face height (`FACE_U`, a share of the body, as `headCrop` takes it) is at least 24 px in a tile; the card's scale is T266's. The pictures are in the repository but **not shipped**: `scripts/copy-assets.js` copies `sets/` and `worlds/` only; T266 adds the copy and the card.

`test:` `test:chargroups` (`scripts/test-character-groups.js`, in `npm test`): one picture per set and none without a set, each a WebP of at most 64 KB without EXIF, XMP or ICC, `FACE_U` equal to `realoffice-layout.ts`'s; where python3 has Pillow, the script's `--selftest` (grid for 1 to 40 people, centred last row, sorted order whatever the key order, the crop inside the picture, the budget walking down and refusing, the scale from the picture, two runs with the same bytes) and `--check` (each committed picture is byte for byte what the set pictures give now). Mutants: `CG_MUTANT=unsorted`, `uncentred`, `unclamped` and `nobudget` each fail the selftest (the test runs them). `read:` every picture was looked at on a contact sheet at 1x and 2x: the sets' people are whole in their squares, no hat, crown or hair cut off, the app's face height is 25 to 29 px of a 96 px tile; the Office people show their raised hands at the tile's sides (a hand is cut at the edge in a few), the Retro people keep their own sizes (one is small, a sprite of its own size). `[KNOWN GAP]` Linux only (Pillow 11.3.0, the version CI pins); Windows and macOS not run, the script uses `os.path` only; Pillow absent, or another version than 11.3.0, skips the rebuild comparison locally; with `DURBAR_REQUIRE_PILLOW=1` a missing or other Pillow fails the test (a self-check runs it with no python and the flag). In CI the test runs in `ci.yml`'s Pillow 11.3.0 venv step (*Art build script selftest*) with `DURBAR_REQUIRE_PILLOW=1`, so the byte comparison runs there under the pin (`real:` CI printed `character-groups: ok`, as relayed by Paco).

**What it replaces:** nothing (FR-303's mock was made outside the repository). **Existing capability checked (FR-031):** `compress-shots.js` is for PNG screenshots (palette quantiser) and cannot read WebP; the sets' own packs are WebP through Pillow (`build-kid-pack.py`, `build-arcade-pack.py`), so this script follows them; `picture-budget.js` (300 KB) still runs on the new files.

**Numbers** (checked 2026-11-13, `npm run specs:numbers`, `origin/main`, every remote branch and open PR head): this entry takes **T343** and no FR (renumbered on 2026-11-13 from the letter name #1704 also used).

## Changes 2026-11-13: T265 is split; the previews are built first as T265a (FR-302, issue #1702, no new FR)

T265 held two pieces of work that share no code: the world previews (FR-302) and the character groups (FR-303, a pure script over shipped pictures). **T265a** (issue #1702) builds the previews; **T265b** (its own issue and pull request) builds the character groups; the test lines are split the same way and FR-302 and FR-303 stand as written. **What T265a does, and the choices it makes where FR-302 left room:**

- `simscene=preview` (simulator only, read from the page address like `simrooms`) replaces the roster by three people of one folder (Ada, Mira, Jonas of `mcp-swiss`), no helpers, every one in Ask first with no restore mark. **All three are idle in every world** (FR-302 allows "done or idle"): an idle person stays at the desk in the Plain office, the Photo office, the cave and, at its cabinet, in the arcade, while a done person leaves for Plain's lounge, and a done turn leaves its own unread mark, so one rule serves all four. The people in the arcade stand beside their cabinets (FR-259's idle frame): that is where the arcade seats an idle person.
- The driver is `scripts/shots-102-previews.js` (in `ui-suite`). Per world it loads `simscene=preview` at 1440x900 and checks the scene on the page itself: three people, three figures at desks, nothing of a slip, notice, banner, toast, bubble or tooltip showing, no scroll, and the world drawn is the one asked for (the cave and the arcade by their `worldflags`, as `shots-102-arcade` does). It then cuts the office area (`#stage`) from the window picture and encodes a **640 px WebP in the page** (Chromium's canvas encoder, so no image tool, no Python, the same on every system; the driver cuts out the ICC chunk the encoder adds, as no shipped picture carries metadata), at the best quality that keeps it at most 48 KB; a picture that cannot fit fails. It runs the same scene at 1280x800 for the check and the look (`PREVIEWS_SHOT=1` keeps both window pictures), but only 1440x900 makes a preview.
- **The zoom origin** (FR-301) is stored with each picture in `previews.json`: the middle of the three figures, in percent of the picture, with the picture's own size and bytes. `test:realoffice-assets` fails a stale size, an origin outside 0..100 and a missing, extra, wide, metadata-bearing or over-48-KB picture.
- **Where they are:** `src/renderer/realoffice/previews/{plain,photo,cave,retro}.webp` and `previews.json`; `scripts/copy-assets.js` ships the pictures and `previews.json` as `previews.js` (`self.DurbarWorldPreviews`; the page's CSP has no `connect-src`, as for the packs) for Settings (T266). The cave and the arcade have a preview although their flags are closed: FR-301 lists a world only when its flag is open, and a picture in the app's files names nothing.
- `npm run shots:docs` retakes them: `DOC_SHOTS` gets an entry with `ship: <folder>`, whose files are written into that folder (or `--out`), as the driver made them, and are exempt from the linked-picture check.

**Measured (Linux, xvfb, simulator):** Plain 11.6 KB, Photo office 33.0 KB, Prehistoric cave 47.3 KB, Retro arcade 45.1 KB. Retakes are not byte-identical between runs (a rerun gave 46.5 KB for the cave against the committed 47.3 KB); the driver lowers the quality until a picture fits and `test:realoffice-assets` checks the cap on the committed bytes, so a retake cannot slip over 48 KB unseen: it fails the driver instead (below a quality of 0.3). `[KNOWN GAP]` Linux only; macOS and Windows fonts draw other plates, so a retake there differs a little. Seen in the pictures, not defects of the previews: in the cave the screens' "Ready. Send a message to start." sits below the stone frame, outside the glass, at 3 people; in the arcade the plates read "Idle · Ready. Send a message to st..." (the cut of FR-311's ellipsis). `test:` `test:realoffice-assets` (and its red cases), `test-compress-shots` (the shipped entry of `shots:docs`); `sim:` `shots-102-previews`.

## Changes 2026-11-13: first build of T290..T293 and T295; a data gap in FR-203 found (issue #1399, no new FR)

Built on `102-290-arcade-default-people`, stacked on the spec PR: the two values (`office`, `pixel`; the second draws the set `retro`) in the lists and in `peopleFor` (FR-320, FR-321, FR-322), Settings (the closed entry and the line), the Photo office's world rules with the Retro pixel set (FR-323, `metaOf`), and the two drivers' cases. Found by building, not by a test failing:
- **FR-203's `faceBox` does not exist for the frames it names.** The Retro pixel set has a `faceBox` per person, but in the standing frame `face-book` (the face seen from the front), not in `typeA`, `waveA` or `rest`; Office people have no `faceBox` at all (their seated frames have `seat.headTop`, `w`, `h`). So Flat's heads (FR-320's and FR-321's "In Flat", T294) need a head crop per set that is defined first (a crop from the seated frame by `seat.headTop` and `w`, found once per person and tested against the picture, as 077 FR-060 did for the crews). `[KNOWN GAP]` until that crop is built: in Flat both values draw the theme's own figures (the Characters list says so in its line only for a saved Retro pixel with its flag closed); T294 and the Flat halves of T290 and T292 stay open.
- The simulator's stand-in for main (`sim.ts`) keeps its own list of the values; it now holds the same ten, typed by the shared list (the unit test compares `theme.js` and `characters.ts`; the driver `check-characters` picks the new values through the stand-in).
- The Characters list shows each set's own first person, cropped to head and shoulders, as the pick preview (the Office people frame is 457x618, so the crop starts at its `headTop`); the closed Retro pixel entry is dimmed as well as disabled and named "Not open yet".

## Changes 2026-11-14: the picture half of T093 is built: `check-world-distinct.js`, its method and limits, and the known-clash list kept honest (FR-016, T093, T097 for three worlds, issue #1402, no new FR)

[FR-014](spec.md), [FR-016](spec.md), SC-011. Owner decision (issue #1400, 2026-11-13): no waiver at the flip, Cave goes to `circle`; until the Cave PR (T174) `worlds/known-clashes.json` stays on main and the checks honour it.

- **Method (agent decision, owner to confirm).** FR-016 left the hash method open. Plain Node has no WebP decoder and the project adds no dependency (Pillow is dev-side only and not on CI), so the pictures are split by what each check needs: (1) **exact copies** are a sha256 of each fixture file's bytes, in plain Node, in `npm test` (`test:world-distinct`, `scripts/test-world-distinct.js`); (2) **near copies and colour** need pixels, which Electron decodes in a hidden window with no app code (`scripts/check-world-distinct.js`, `npm run check:world-distinct`, run in CI by the World matrix subset and weekly jobs, and by hand under the Electron lock; the npm script starts it through `scripts/run-electron-script.js`, which puts `--no-sandbox` on Electron's command line on Linux only (a switch set inside the app is too late: Chromium checks the SUID sandbox first), as `world-matrix.js` passes it; a watchdog stops it with a message naming the step if one decode takes over 30 s or the whole run over 60 s, about 50 times the 1 s it takes; precedent `scripts/build-icons.js`; it loads no app page, so `test-ui-suite.ts` lists it with the files that are not page drivers); the comparison itself is pure (`scripts/world-distinct.js`) and is tested on planted pixels in plain Node. A fixture is `fx-*.webp`, `desk*.webp` and `monitor*.webp`; the colour roles are `wall.webp`, `floor.webp` and `desk.webp`. Every fixture of a world is compared with every fixture of every other world, whatever its name, so a renamed copy is found.
- **Near copies:** two hashes, both must be near. A **silhouette hash** (alpha at 16x16 cells, 256 bits) and a **difference hash** (9x8 cells of the mean of the three channels, composited over mid-grey, 64 positions each lighter, darker or level with its right neighbour, a step under 3 of 255 counts as level so re-encoding noise does not flip it; the light and dark swapped counts as the same, so an inverted copy is near). The mean of the channels, not the weighted luminance, so swapping or rotating the colour channels leaves the hash unchanged. A copy flipped left to right is found too: both hashes are also taken against the mirrored picture (each row reversed, lighter and darker swapped) and the nearer counts. A picture whose cells hardly vary and which is wholly opaque or wholly clear is `flat` and is skipped by the hashes (it has no shape; an identical file is still a copy), so many tiny solid pictures are not one big clash.
- **Colour:** the average colour (alpha-weighted, sRGB to Lab) of wall, floor and desk: a wall, floor or desk pair whose average colours are within delta E 8 (CIE76) of each other fails.
- **Limits, from the real pairs and planted copies (2026-11-14, Electron on macOS):** difference hash at most 8 of 64 **and** silhouette at most 24 of 256 is near; delta E at most 8 is the same colour. Real pairs, nearest fixtures by both hashes: cave and photo 13/64 and 27/256, cave and retro 17/64 and 40/256, photo and retro 22/64 and 42/256 (taking the mirror into account; a first measure without it gave 14, 17 and 30; the closest silhouette of any pair without the mirror was 18, its difference hash 30). Real average colours: floors cave (91,66,48), photo (204,173,147), retro (244,230,207); the closest real pair is the photo and retro floors, delta E 20.3, the desks 21.7 and over. Planted copies of real fixtures: a channel-rotated copy 0/64 and 0/256, an inverted copy 1/64 and 0/256, a mirrored copy 3/64 and 0/256, a 70 percent re-encode at quality 35, 2/64 and 1/256; a floor of photo's mean colour, delta E 0.3. So the difference-hash limit has a margin of 5 bits on the nearest real pair (cave and photo, whose silhouette is also only 3 over: both must move for it to fail) and planted copies sit 5 or more under it; colour has 12. `test:world-distinct` pins the limits and these measured distances (a small table in the test, not decoded), so a limit moved to the cave and photo pair fails in `npm test`. **T097's measure-and-plant is done here for the three existing worlds** (the limits fail now, not only report); the box stays unticked until the owner confirms, and the limits move only by evidence (097 FR-190).
- **`worlds/known-clashes.json`.** It lists a card clash. `check-world-distinct` and `test:world-catalogue` read it through the shared `scripts/world-cards.js` (the card logic moved out of `test-world-catalogue.js`, no change in what it checks): a listed pair is reported in one line (`[KNOWN GAP] cave and photo share the same arrangement`), a picture clash is never waived by it, any clash that is not listed fails, and a listed pair that **no longer clashes on its field fails** ("remove it from known-clashes.json"), so the PR that makes Cave `circle` has to delete the entry.
- **Mutants (killed at their own label):** a copied fixture under another name (COPY), a channel-rotated copy and an inverted copy (RECOLOURED), a blurred and nudged copy and a copy at twice the size (RE-ENCODED), a floor of the same average colour (COLOUR), the limits at and just over each bound, and a stale known-clash entry.
- **In CI.** It needs Electron, so it is not in `npm test`: `world-matrix.yml` runs `xvfb-run -a npm run check:world-distinct` after the selftest in its subset and weekly jobs, with path filters for `worlds/**` and the scripts (added by Paco's account, commit 9c4f90813; the agents' tokens have no workflow scope). Green on Linux in World matrix run 43538940245: the `[KNOWN GAP]` line for cave and photo and `check-world-distinct OK (3 worlds ...; 8/64 difference-hash bits, 24/256 silhouette bits, delta E 8)`. Not verified: macOS and Windows runs in CI (the job is Linux).
- **Not built:** the contact sheet and `distinct-check` (T094, the next PR). Rotated or cropped copies are not looked for (`[KNOWN GAP]`; a mirrored one is).

## Changes 2026-11-14: the contact sheet of pictures and the required `distinct-check` field (FR-017, T094, issue #1402, no new FR)

[FR-017](spec.md), [FR-035](spec.md), SC-011. Built on the picture check of the entry above (stacked on its PR).

- **`scripts/world-contact-sheet.js`** (`npm run world:contact-sheet`, Electron, dev-side, under the Electron lock; started through `scripts/run-electron-script.js`, which adds `--no-sandbox` on Linux only, and a 60 s watchdog stops it, as for `check-world-distinct`) draws one row per world: the card's arrangement id, monitor, mood and light as the heading, then wall, floor, desk, monitor and every fixture picture (never the crew's kid pictures) at one 120 px box on a neutral grey, labelled by file name, and writes one JPEG to `out/world-contact-sheet/` (git-ignored, never committed, not in `DOC_SHOTS`: 098 FR-054). The layout is pure and tested in plain Node (`test:world-contact-sheet`: every picture once, none outside the sheet, no overlap, wrapped rows, real worlds).
- **Not built (stays open, T094 unticked):** the sheet FR-017 describes shows each world in the simulator scene at about 320x200, so the seating plan can be judged; this one shows the pictures and names the arrangement. The scene part needs the matrix scene (`world-matrix.js`, FR-296) and is the next step; until it exists the reviewer judges the seating plan from the card and the simulator, and records only what was looked at.
- **Where the record lives (agent decision, the owner may change it): `worlds/<id>/card.json`, field `distinct-check`**. **This replaces FR-017's "in the new world's `ASSETS.md`" with "in the world's `card.json`, field `distinct-check`", and FR-035(g)'s citation of it; both texts are changed in this entry.** Why: the catalogue test already reads every card as data, and `ASSETS.md` is free text that would need a parser. It is `{ "state": "pending" }` until a person has looked at the sheet, then `{ "state": "passed", "by": <name>, "date": "YYYY-MM-DD", "worlds": <how many were compared, at least 2> }`; no other state or field. A pending look names no reviewer. `test:world-catalogue` fails a card without the field or with a malformed one (fixtures: missing, unknown state, no reviewer, blank reviewer, impossible date, other date shape, fewer than two worlds, count as text, extra field, a pending one with a reviewer). **Nothing sets `passed` but a person editing the card after looking:** the three existing cards are `pending`, because nobody has recorded a look; the owner's look is `read:` and is not claimed here. T036's availability gate will refuse a world whose state is not `passed`.
- **`[KNOWN GAP]` owner to confirm the move.** The replacement is an agent decision; Vikram must confirm that the record lives in `card.json` and not in `ASSETS.md`, and either of them may move it back (then FR-017 and FR-035(g) change again).
- **Text on the sheet is measured, not assumed:** a picture name, a heading and a monitor and mood line are cut with an ellipsis to the width of their box (the canvas measures them), so none runs into the next picture or past the sheet; `test:world-contact-sheet` plans the text boxes and fails one that leaves the sheet or overlaps another.
- **What the sheet showed on 2026-11-14 (the agent's own look, not the owner's):** Cave is a warm brown and orange set of hand-drawn stone things, Photo is pale oak and glass photographs, Retro is dark neon pixel art cabinets; no two worlds' pictures can be mistaken at this size. The headings show what the sheet cannot fix: Cave and Photo are both `rows`, the known clash of the entry above.

## Changes 2026-11-14: the strips of #1625 and the narrow plate's font of #1626, measured on Linux (T345, issues #1625, #1626, no new FR)

[FR-229](spec.md) and FR-293 hold with the restore, away, crash and follow strips too, not only the MCP strip, on Linux; the narrow plate's room holds in every Linux font tried. Measurement only, plus a driver case and a font line: no product change. **Measured** on Linux (Xvfb, Electron under the simulator, Retro arcade, 15 agents, `simkinds=mixed`, 13 plates), each strip alone: restore, crash, follow and MCP 31 px, away 36 px, crash list open 68 px, MCP list open 221 px (1440x900) and 226 px (1280x800). **Floor (clientHeight) and result:** 1440x900 with no strip 852 px; one strip of 31 px 821 px, away 816 px; away plus follow or restore plus away 785 px, no lend wanted. 1280x800 with no strip 752 px; one strip 721 px, away 716 px (room 697 and 692 px, hall needs 682: no lend); away plus follow and restore plus away 685 px with the #1573 lend taken (`ro-tight`, hall 685 px: 3 px over the 682 px it needs). No scroll in any of these. **Scrolls (already `[KNOWN GAP]` of #1589, now with Linux numbers):** three strips at once (restore, away and MCP, or crash, or follow) at 1280x800 (floor 654 px against a hall of 700 px); the MCP list open at both sizes; the crash list open (68 px) with the restore strip at 1280x800. The crash list open alone passes at both sizes (1280x800 with the lend). A strip a pixel taller on Linux is not found: Linux's strips are 31 px as on macOS, the away strip is 36 px and passes. **Narrow plate (#1626, FR-311, FR-312), Linux:** `shots-102-arcade` with `ARCADE_CASES=narrow`, "Limit reached" at 10.5 px: Noto Sans (what `system-ui` resolved to on this machine) word 67.1 px, room 17.05 px (a failed helper) and 19.88 px (none); with the font forced, DejaVu Sans 71.9 px and 7.39 px (the coordinator's earlier number, reproduced), Liberation Sans 63.0 px and 21.06 px, Ubuntu 64.0 px and 20.22 px. Content box 99 px, padding 3 and 18 px, and "+N!" whole in each; the room is above the 3 px floor in all four. **Change:** `shots-058-mcp-launch` `DRIVER_WORLD=retro DRIVER_CASE=strips` (T345) asserts no scroll for each of restore, away, crash and follow alone and for away plus follow and restore plus away, at both sizes, and prints the heights; `shots-102-arcade` prints once which font the narrow plate's word is drawn in (the stack, the drawn width, the same word's canvas width in each installed candidate, the nearest). `sim:` the case fails with the lend taken out (`lend: 0` in `app.js`): "arcade away+follow strip 1280x800: the office does not scroll (scrollHeight 724 against clientHeight 685)". **Not verified, `[KNOWN GAP]`:** Windows (Segoe UI and Windows strip heights; needs a Windows machine); the font the Linux CI runner picks for `system-ui` is not named until a CI log carries the new line (inferred only: the runner image is not this machine; DejaVu Sans, the widest tried here, still leaves 7.39 px); a Mac was not rerun; Segoe UI's width is not claimed.
